【大盤指數/股價】42712 / 348.5

➢ 3Q26 獲利略低於投研部預估 
公司 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元，YoY+9.7%，營收略低於投研部預估之
106.8 億元，主要係因成衣營收低於投研部預期所致，毛利率 31.18%，
優於投研部預期，主要受惠高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比提升，且匯率
亦有所助益(約影響毛利率 0.62 個百分點)，費用率因業績獎金提列較高
而推升至 10.49%，營益率 20.69%，低於投研部預估之 21.24%，稅後
EPS 6.36 元，低於投研部預估之 6.57 元。 
➢ 4Q26 展望正向，略為上修 4Q26 獲利預估 
10/2026 營收 34 億元，YoY+10.7%，符合投研部預期，本次法說會管
理層表示對 4Q26 營運維持 TITAS 2026 釋出之看法不變，4Q26 營收可
望為年內高峰，上修 4Q26 營收預估至 105.99 億元，YoY+14.4%，毛
利率因原料成本負面影響趨緩，公司高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比持續
提升，毛利率同步上修至 31.05%，YoY+0.86ppt.，預估稅後淨利 17.25
億元，YoY+9%，稅後 EPS 上修至 6.29 元。目前訂單能見度維持 6 個
月，展望正向，期中選舉民主黨勝選預期對關稅壁壘政策可望有所趨緩，
且美伊戰爭亦可望有所緩解，市場不確定因素減少亦有利於整體經濟消
費動能，印尼廠一期、二期目前維持穩定獲利且產能滿載，投研部上修
2026 年營收預估至 408.77 億元，YoY+7.6%，毛利率因新產品、新客
戶營收占比持續提升，略為上修至 31.09%，YoY+2.45ppt.，稅後 EPS
上修至 24.99 元。  
➢ 2027 年營運成長可期，評價處歷史區間低緣且殖利率逾 5%  
公司目前始承接 04/2027 訂單，觀察美國總體經濟動能強勁，消費者支
出可望維持韌性，惟考量公司現有廠區產能趨於滿載，目前僅以原有產
能小規模擴充為主，投研部以過去幾年公司中性成長均值預估 2027 年
營收 431.95 億元，YoY+5.7%，毛利率因 4Q27 印尼新廠投產而略為承
壓，預估 2027 年毛利率 30.62%，YoY-0.47ppt.，營益率預估 20.74%，
YoY+0.01ppt.，稅後 EPS 預估由 26.19 元略為上修至 26.31 元，投研部
以現金股利配發率 74.63%預估，現金股利殖利率可望達 5.35%，且依
未來四季合計稅後 EPS 估算目前評價仍處過往歷史區間低緣位置，長期
投資價值浮現，投研部維持評價 PER 16X 並以未來四季稅後 EPS 估算目
標價，目標價由 396 元上修至 416 元(4Q26~3Q27F PE 16X)，維持買
進投資建議。

4Q26 展望正向，略為上修 4Q26 獲利預估 
10/2026 營收 34 億元，MoM-3.1%，YoY+10.7%，YTD+5.9%，占投研部原預估
4Q26 營收達成率 33.4%，符合投研部預期，公司過去 10 月單月營收占 4Q26 營收
占比區間介於 29.5%~34.2%，本次法說會管理層表示對 4Q26 營運維持台北紡織展
(TITAS 2026)釋出之看法不變，以訂單來看，若出貨船期順利，則 4Q26 營收可望為
年內高 峰， 基此 ，投研 部 上修 4Q26 營收 預估 至 105.99 億 元，QoQ+0.3%，
YoY+14.4%，毛利率因原料成本負面影響趨緩，公司高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比持
續提升，毛利率亦同步上修至 31.05%，QoQ-0.14ppt.，YoY+0.86ppt.，並同步上修
營益率至 20.33%，QoQ-0.36ppt.，YoY+1.17ppt.，預估稅後淨利 17.25 億元，QoQ-
1.1%，YoY+9%，稅後 EPS 由 5.84 元略為上修至 6.29 元。 
展望 2026 年，訂單能見度維持 2 個季度，訂單展望維持正向，公司表示目前訂單尚
未因美國期中選舉結果出爐後而有所變動，然期中選舉民主黨勝選預期對關稅壁壘政
策可望有所趨緩，且美伊戰爭亦可望有所緩解，市場不確定因素減少亦有利於整體經
濟消費動能，印尼廠一期、二期目前維持穩定獲利且產能滿載，目前協力廠外發比重
維持 27%~28%，因 4Q26 營收及獲利略為上修，投研部亦同步上修 2026 年營收預
估至 408.77 億元，YoY+7.6%，毛利率部份，因高毛利率新產品、新客戶營收占比可
望 持 續 提 升 ， 且 匯 率 環 境 相 較 於 2025 年 亦 較 為 友 善 ， 略 為 上 修 31.09% ，
YoY+2.45ppt.，營益率則由 20.62%上修至 20.73%，YoY+2.06ppt.，並預估稅後淨
利 68.56 億元，YoY+24.3%，稅後 EPS 由 24.77 元上修至 24.99 元。

展望 2027 年，目前已開始承接 04/2027 訂單，雖訂單能見度未達 2027 年底，且客
戶端 2027 年預示單仍洽談中，然觀察美國就業環境仍穩健，初領及續領失業救濟金
人數仍處歷史低檔，總體經濟動能強勁，消費者支出可望維持韌性，且 2026 年營運
受戰爭、通膨影響較為劇烈，研判 2027 年可望較為淡化，惟考量公司現有廠區產能
趨於滿載，印尼三期實際貢獻營收時點落於 4Q27~1Q28，目前僅以原有產能小規模
擴充產能為主，若市況優於預期則以外發協力廠因應，2027 年外發比重大致上介於

億元，YoY+5.7%，主要為產品報價提升之貢獻，並預估毛利率因 4Q27 印尼新廠投

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 11 日

1 群益投顧 
台積電(2330 TT)投資 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 延續過去參與 Arm(ARM US) IPO 的少數
策 略 股 權 模 式 ， 惟 布 局 由 半 導 體 供 給 生 態 系 進 一 步 延 伸 至 AI 模 型 與 算 力 需 求 端 。 透
過投資 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 各最高 5 億 美 元 ， 增 加 對 未 來 算 力 需 求 及 運 算 架 構 演 進
的 掌 握 ， 作 為 先 進 製 程 與 封 裝 產 能 規 劃 的 參 考 ， 但 不 代 表 取 得 訂 單 或 控 制 權 。 現在台
積 電 的 產 能 和 良 率 優 於 同 業 ， 可是未來美 國 有 可 能 因 環 境 能 耗 與 安 全 疑 慮 對 AI 數據
中 心 及 尖 端 模 型 祭 出 強 硬 監 管 ， 並 取 消 稅 收 補 貼 ， 引 發 市 場 擔 憂 科 技 巨 頭 放 緩 AI 資
本 支 出 ， 間 接 影 響 台 積 電 的 業 績 成 長 動 能 ， 因 此 建 議 台 積 電 Trading Buy。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw

台 積 電 並 非 首 次 策 略 性 投 資 產 業 鏈 公 司 ， 惟 此 次 首 度 將 布 局 延 伸 至 AI 算力
需 求 端 ：  
台積電於 11/10/2026 召 開 董 事 會 ， 分 別 核 准 兩 項 投 資 案 ： 一 是 以 不 超 過 美
金 5 億 元 設 立 投 資 公 司，持 續 進 行 半 導 體 產 業 及 相 關 生 態 系 之 策 略 性 投 資；
二 是 分 別 以 不 超 過 美 金 5 億 元 認 購 Anthropic, PBC 普 通 股 及 OpenAI Group 
PBC 特 別 股，兩 項 直 接 股 權 投 資 合 計 最 高 10 億 美 元。其 中，Anthropic 每股
認購價格將依該公司未來首次公開發行(IPO) 價格而定。值得注意的是，新
設 投 資 公 司 與 直 接 投 資 兩 家 AI 模 型 業 者 為 兩 項 獨 立 的 董 事 會 決 議 ， 目 前 並
無公開資訊顯示兩者具有直接的資金或架構關聯，整體反映台積電一方面
持 續 布 局 半 導 體 產 業 生 態 系 ， 另 一 方 面 首 度 將 直 接 股 權 投 資 延 伸 至 AI 模型
與 算 力 需 求 形 成 端 。  
從歷史脈絡觀察，台積電早在 1990 年 代 即 透 過 InveStar 及 VentureTech 
Alliance(VTA) 等 創 投 架 構 投 資 全 球 無 晶 圓 廠 (Fabless)及 新 興 科 技 公 司，以及
入股 IMS Nanofabrication 約 10%以 布 局 關 鍵 多 重 電 子 束 光 罩 技 術 。 其 中 ，
2023 年 以 不 超 過 1 億 美 元 參 與 Arm IPO 是 此 次 投資最 具 參 考 價 值 的 案 例 ，
台 積 電 當 時 並 非 為 取 得 經 營 控 制 權 ， 而 是 以 少 數 策 略 股 權 維 持 與 關 鍵 IP 生
態 系 的 長 期 連 結 。 此 次 對 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 各 最 高 5 億 美 元 的 投 資 ， 本
質上延續了此種非控制性少數策略股權模式，共同反映台積電並非以大額
資 本 主 導 標 的 ， 而 是 透 過 有 限 資 本 強 化 與 關 鍵 生 態 系 夥 伴 的 策 略 連 結 。  
最 大 戰 略 意 義 ： 策 略 股 權 由 半 導 體 供 給 端 進 一 步 延 伸 至 AI 算 力 需 求 端 ：  
過 去 台 積 電 的 策 略 投 資 主 要 集 中 於 晶 片 設 計 、 半 導 體 IP 及 關 鍵 設 備 技 術 ，
核 心 目 的 在 於 擴 大 半 導 體 供 給 生 態 系，協 助 客 戶 持 續 開 發 並 製 造 先 進 晶 片，
投資 Arm 即 屬 於 間 接 支 持 相 關 架 構 設 計 與 製 造 需 求 的 模 式 。  
相 較 之 下 ， OpenAI 及 Anthropic 位於 AI 產 業 鏈 的 需 求 形 成 端 ， 模 型 能 力 提
升 、 訓 練 規 模 擴 大 及 推 論 需 求 增 加 ， 最 終 將 帶 動 GPU、 ASIC、 HBM 及 資 料
中 心 基 礎 設 施 的 算 力 需 求。因 此，投 資 2 家 模 型 業 者 的 策 略 意 義 並 非 直 接 取
得 晶 圓 訂 單 ， 而 是 增 加 台 積 電 接 觸 及 理 解 未 來 AI 算 力 需 求 變 化 的 機 會 ， 包
括訓練(Training) 與推論(Inference) 需 求 結 構、運 算 架 構 演 進 及 資 料 中 心 基 礎
設 施 需 求 等 ， 作 為 長 期 先 進 製 程 及 先 進 封 裝 產 能 規 劃 的 參 考 。  
相 較 台 積 電 2026 年 預 估 600 億至 640 億 美 元 的 資 本 支 出 規 模 ， 新 設 投 資 公
司的 5 億 美 元 資 金 上 限 僅 約 0.8%；而 直 接 投 資 兩 家 模 型 業 者 合 計 最 高 10 億
美 元 ， 相 較 2 者 各 自 超 過 1,000 億 美 元 的 資 金 規 模 及 高 估 值 亦 屬 有 限 。  
若與 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 既 有 主 要 策 略 投 資 者 相 比 ， 台 積 電 各 最 高 5 億美

### Page 2

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 11 日

2 群益投顧 
元 的 投 資 規 模 明 顯 較 小 。 過 去 Microsoft(MSFT US)、SoftBank(9984 JP)等業
者對 OpenAI 的 投 資 金 額 達 數 十 億 甚 至 百 億 美 元 級 別 ； Amazon(AMZN US)
及 Google(GOOL US) 對 Anthropic 的 累 計 投 資 亦 達 數 十 億 美 元 。 相 較 之 下 ，
台 積 電 此 次 各 5 億 美 元 的 投 資，顯 然 並 非 以 資 金 主 導，取 得 控 制 權，董 事 會
席次、主 導 公 司 經 營 為 或 追 求 短 期 財 務 回 報 為 目 的，而 是 以 相 對 有 限 的 資 本
建 立 策 略 關 係。惟 實 際 持 股 比 例 仍 須 視 認 購 價 格、股 權 條 件 及 後 續 稀 釋 情 況
而定，目前亦無資訊顯示台積電因此取得參與模型公司技術決策或運算架
構 (Compute Roadmap) 制 定 的 權 利 。  
對 台 積 電 而 言 ， 投 資 AI 模 型 業 者 亦 有 助 於 擴 大 觀 察 未 來 算 力 需 求 的 來 源 ，
而 非 完 全 依 賴 單 一 晶 片 供 應 商 的 產 品 規 劃。無 論 未 來 模 型 業 者 採 用 NVIDIA 
GPU、 AMD GPU、 Google TPU 、 AWS Trainium 或其他自研 ASIC， 只 要 相
關晶片及先進封裝採用台積電技術，即可能轉化為台積電的製程與封裝需
求 ， 策 略 價 值 在 於 增 加 對 需 求 端 運 算 架 構 發 展 方 向 的 理 解 。  
仍 須 面 對 客 戶 中 立 性 、 訂 單 轉 換 及 股 權 評 價 等 風 險 ：  
這 項 投 資 案，仍 有 幾 項 風 險。首 先，台 積 電 必 須 維 持 客 戶 中 立 性。OpenAI 及
Anthropic 分 別 與 Microsoft、Google 及 Amazon 等 大 型 雲 端 業 者 存 在 密 切 合
作 關 係 ， 並 涉 及 NVIDIA、 AMD、 Broadcom 及其他 AI 晶 片 供 應 鏈 。 台 積 電
直接投資特定模型業者，可能使部分客戶關注其資訊隔離及資源配置的中
立 性。此 次 採 取 各 最 高 5 億 美 元 的 少 數 股 權 投 資，且 同 時 投 資 兩 家 主 要 模 型
業 者，有 助 降 低 市 場 對 單 一 選 邊 的 疑 慮，但 台 積 電 後 續 仍 須 維 持 嚴 格 的 商 業
資 訊 保 密 及 客 戶 中 立 原 則 。  
其 次 ， 股 權 投 資 不 等 於 取 得 訂 單 或 產 能 承 諾 。 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 並非直
接 採 購 晶 圓 的 客 戶，其 算 力 需 求 仍 須 透 過 CSP、GPU 及 ASIC 供 應 商 轉 化 為
台 積 電 的 晶 圓 與 先 進 封 裝 需 求。因 此，這 筆 投 資 本 身 並 不 代 表 產 能 長 約、保
證取貨(Take-or-Pay) 承 諾 或 未 來 訂 單 保 證 ， 實 際 效 益 仍 取 決 於 AI 算 力 需 求
的 持 續 成 長 ， 以 及 相 關 晶 片 供 應 鏈 採 用 台 積 電 技 術 的 程 度 。  
最後，AI 模 型 業 者 目 前 仍 處 於 高 資 本 投 入 及 快 速 競 爭 階 段 。 若 未 來 模 型 服
務 競 爭 加 劇、商 業 化 速 度 低 於 預 期，或 雲 端 業 者 的 AI 資 本 支 出 回 報 率 下 降，
導 致 算 力 投 資 放 緩 ， 均 可 能 影 響 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 的 估 值 及 後 續 融 資 能
力 ， 進 而 帶 來 台 積 電 股 權 投 資 的 評 價 波 動 風 險 。  
整 體 而 言，台 積 電 此 次 策 略 並 非 改 變 晶 圓 代 工 本 業，而 是 延 續 過 去 以 少 數 策
略 股 權 連 結 關 鍵 產 業 生 態 系 的 做 法 。 若 說 投 資 Arm 主 要 是 鞏 固 晶 片 如 何 被
設 計 與 製 造 的 供 給 生 態 系 ， 投 資 OpenAI 與 Anthropic 則 是 進 一 步 接 觸 為 何
需 要 更 多 晶 片 與 算 力 的 需 求 形 成 端。對 台 積 電 而 言，以 相 對 有 限 的 資 本 投 入
增加對 AI 產 業 長 期 需 求 變 化 的 理 解 ， 其 策 略 意 義 可 能 高 於 單 純 的 財 務 投 資
報酬。 
11/2026 台 積 電 董 事 會 決 議 如 下 ：  
11/11/2026 台 積 電 召 開 董 事 會 ， 會 中 重 要 決 議 如 下 ：  
1)核准 3Q26 營 業 報 告 書 及 財 務 報 表 ， 3Q26 合 併 營 收 約 新 台 幣 1.49 兆 元 ，
稅 後 純 益 約 新 台 幣 7,630 億 元 ， 稅 後 EPS 29.43 元。 
2)核准配發 3Q26 每 股 現 金 股 利 9.0 元 。 台 積 電 1Q26 和 2Q26 各分派 7 元，

3 群益投顧 
3Q26 發 9 元，依 據 台 積 電 股 利 只 升 不 降，4Q26 至 少 配 發 9 元，也 就 是 2026
年將配發 32 元 以 上 。  
3)核 准 資 本 預 算 約 美 金 409.27 億 元，內 容 主 要 包 括：1.建 置 及 升 級 先 進 製 程
產 能 ；2.建 置 及 升 級 先 進 封 裝 、 成 熟 及 /或 特 殊 製 程 產 能 ；3.廠 房 興 建 及 廠 務
設 施 工 程 ； 4. 2027 年 研 發 資 本 預 算 、 經 常 性 資 本 預 算 及 資 本 化 租 賃 資 產 預
算。 
4)核 准 以 不 超 過 美 金 5 億 元 之 資 金 設 立 一 投 資 公 司，以 進 行 半 導 體 產 業 及 相
關 生 態 系 之 策 略 性 投 資 。  
5)核 准 於 不 超 過 美 金 5 億 元 之 額 度 內 認 購 Anthropic, PBC 普 通 股 股 份，每 股
認 購 價 格 將 依 該 公 司 首 次 公 開 發 行 之 價 格 而 定 ； 以 及 於 不 超 過 美 金 5 億元
之 額 度 內 認 購 OpenAI Group PBC 特 別 股 股 份 。  
台 積 電 10/2026 營收 5,868.9 億元，MoM+14.65%， YoY+59.70%：  
台積電公告 10/2026 營收 5,868.90 億 元 ， MoM+14.65%， YoY+59.70%，創
歷 史 新 高 。 先 前 台 積 電 法 說 會 預估 4Q26 營收 523~543 億美元，
QoQ+11.94%~+16.22%，中 間 值 +14.08%。在 1 美元兌 新 台 幣 31.8 元 基 礎 上，
新 台 幣 營 收 為 1.66 兆元~1.73 兆元，QoQ+13.43%~+15.56% 。 以 此 推 估 ，
11/2026 和 12/2026 單 月 平 均 營 收 在 5,381 億元~5,699 億 元 ， MoM-2.89%~-
8.31%。  
高 階 製 程 持 續 帶 動 台 積 電 營 收 成 長 ， 台 積 電 預 估 4Q26 毛 利 率 將 介 於 65%-
67%，以 中 間 值 66%計 算，較 3Q26 下降約 0.4 個 百 分 點。主 要 是 因 為 N2 技
術 的 快 速 爬 坡，預 期 將 稀 釋 毛 利 率 約 3 個 百 分 點，此 稀 釋 將 被 領 先 技 術 的 強
勁需求與持續的成本改善( 含生產力提升) 部分抵消。預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 
32.64 元。2026 年 EPS 111.39 元。 
儘 管 消 費 性 ( 如 PC、 智 慧 型 手 機 ) 與 價 格 敏 感 終 端 市 場 面 臨 零 組 件 價 格 上 漲
與 總 體 經 濟 不 確 定 性 之 挑 戰 ， 台 積 電 對 2027 年 對 此 抱 持 審 慎 樂 觀 態 度 ， 但
受惠於領先先進製程之技術差異化與廣泛客戶基礎，及代理人 AI(Agentic 
AI) 推動 AI 助 理 普 及 、 開 源 模 型 降 低 使 用 門 檻 ， 進 一 步 刺 激 CPU 與 網 路 晶
片 需 求 ， 預 估 台 積 電 2027 年 稅 後 EPS 147.10 元 。 長 期 而 言 ， 台 積 電 憑 藉 在
先 進 製 程 與 先 進 封 裝 的 高 市 佔 率，實 質 基 本 面 依 然 強 勁。然 而，美 國 選 舉 後，
有 可 能 因 環 境 能 耗 與 安 全 疑 慮 對 AI 數 據 中 心 及 尖 端 模 型 祭 出 強 硬 監 管 ， 並
取 消 稅 收 補 貼 ， 引 發 市 場 擔 憂 科 技 巨 頭 放 緩 AI 資 本 支 出 ， 間 接 影 響 台 積 電
的 業 績 成 長 動 能 ， 因 此 建 議 台 積 電 Trading Buy 。

4 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

AllRing Tech Co.  | Asia Pacific
3Q26   margin miss from product
mix
3Q26 earnings missed our estimate by 13% and consensus by 16%, given less
favorable product mix...   There was less  inspection (gross margin >60%) but more
automation tools (gross margin ~40%).
...as well as weaker subsidiary company performance.
Looking into 4Q26, we expect gross margin to recover to >50% from improving
product mix: However, this will still be slightly offset by new product shipped to

the OSATs, which carries lower margin in the early ramping phase.
We think CoWoS should still account for 70-80% of 2027e total revenue – with
other new customers and tools, including a US customer, CPO, and CoW process.
We think CPO could account for 15-20% of 2027e revenue, with shipments up >10x
from 2026.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AllRing Tech Co. (6187.TWO)
Base case, residual income model. Our key assumptions are:
n Cost of equity (CoE) of 8%, derived from a risk-free rate of 2.0%, beta of 1.0, and a
market risk premium of 6.0%.
n Intermediate growth rate of 16%.
n Cash dividend payout of 65%
n Terminal growth rate of 4.5%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion
n Faster-than-expected adoption of silicon photonics

n Market share gains from peers, led by continuous R&D and China's localization trend
Risks to Downside
n Slowdown in CoWoS capacity expansion
n Allring's "WoS" market share declining
n Slowdown in technology migration such as SoIC and silicon photonics adoption

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Kinik

Strong advanced nodes and packaging 
expansion support decent earnings

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,050 TWD | Price: 737.00 TWD

3Q26 margin beat on favorable product mix  
Kinik’s 3Q26 sales grew 8% QoQ, mainly driven by solid diamond disk demand from N2 
and advanced packaging capacity ramp and increasing shipments of high-end 
test/reclaim wafer, more than offsetting weakness at Thailand ABU subsidiary. 3Q26 
GMs improved by 220bps QoQ to 42.2% on improving product mix across ABU and DBU 
segments, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$3.91(vs. BofAe/Street NT$4.09/NT$4.08).  
4Q26 sales grow on continued advanced nodes expansion 
The company guided 4Q26 sales to grow slightly QoQ (BofAe + 2% QoQ), implying full-
year revenue growth of +25% YoY, backed by strength across diamond disk, test wafer, 
and reclaim wafer amid ongoing advanced nodes capacity build-out. Strong contribution 
from high-margin DBU segment, coupled with a favorable mix shift in ABU driven by 
ABF diamond wheels, should lift 4Q26 GMs further to 43.5%, in our view.  
Solid DBU demand and SBU ramp underpin '27-28  
On accelerated 3nm, 2nm and A16 capacity ramp at full utilization, alongside benign 
mature node supply/demand landscape and steady engagement with Intel on 18A/14A, 
we forecast Kinik’s diamond disk shipment to +48% CAGR over '26-28. Meanwhile, 
Kinik’s SBU sales could deliver +22% CAGR over '26-28 as 10% price hike on selected 
products take effect from early 2027 and carrier wafer demand from Micron HBM grow 
more meaningfully from 2H27. Beyond its existing business, grinding wheels for 
advanced packaging are currently under customer qualification, and would provide 
upside potential with TAM much larger than diamond disk.  
Diverse drivers and margin expansion warrant re-rating 
We keep our 2026/27/28 EPS largely unchanged at NT$14.98/26.23/36.84. Reiterate our 
Buy rating and PO of NT$1,050, still based on 32x 2H27-1H28E P/E (vs. its historical 
range 9-42x), supported by robust sales/earnings CAGR of +32%/+57% over '26-28.

Company Sector 
Semiconductor Capital Equipment

Company Description 
Kinik was established in 1953 as a conventional grinding 
wheels supplier. Since 1990-2000s, the company has 
transformed itself into a semiconductor consumables 
supplier including CMP pad conditioners (diamond disks) 
and reclaim/test wafers.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Kinik. We acknowledge its 
technology for CMP diamond disk is solid to support its 
gain in TSMC advanced nodes and Intel along with growing 
reclaimed and testing wafer demand. We expect to see 
margins expansion from mix upgrade in coming years.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Kinik (KIKCF) 
Our Price Objective of NT$1,050 is based on 32x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at upper 
half of the company's historical trading range of 9-42x. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is underpinned by 
1) around +57% earnings CAGR in 2026-28E supported by +32% sales CAGR growth and 
GMs/OpMs expansion, 2) ROE expansion to around 30% in 2025-28E, 4) net cash of 
around NT$30/share, and 5) free cash flow generation of around NT$50/share per 
annum across 2025-27E.

Downside risks to our estimates and expectations are 
1) weaker market demand, especially in the cloud AI investment moderation, leading to 
lower utilization across its major foundry and memory customers and undermining the 
company's revenue growth and operating leverage 
2) slower manufacturing technology migration by its foundry and memory customers, 
dragging the product upgrade cycle for Kinik and potentially spurring more competition 
from its peers. 
3) failure to execute or delay of technology roadmap, potentially leading to an order shift 
to its competitors.

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test

STRESS TEST 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

목표주가 37만원, ‘28년 기준으로는 56만원까지 열림 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 37만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3배를 적용했다. 이는 ‘00년 이후 25년간 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 
해당한다[표1]. ‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 56%, 59%, 48%로 지난 25년 평균인 
22%를 크게 상회할 전망이며, 고원형 ROE가 장기간 유지될 것으로 판단한다.  
이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해질 경우 시장의 시야는 ‘28년으로 향할 것이다. 
이 경우 ‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 56만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 43% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -41%를 넘어섰다. 특히 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
이러한 우려가 지속되는 한, 이익의 눈높이가 높아질수록 멀티플은 저렴해질 
뿐이며, DRAM 가격이 예상보다 더 올라갈 것이라는 소식이 주가의 촉매로 
작용하지 못한다. 결국 주가 반등을 위해서는 시장이 상정하고 있는 다운사이클이 
우려만큼 깊지 않을 것이라는 믿음이 필요한 것으로 본다.

STRESS TEST와 HBM 재평가 
① 당사는 고객들이 어느 정도 가격대에서 LTA를 해지하려는 유인이 생길지 
추정해봤다. Micron의 코멘트를 차용하여 ‘22~’23년 수준의 스트레스 테스트를 
시나리오 분석한 결과, 4Q27 고점 대비 DRAM 가격이 60% 수준 빠져야 해지가 
검토될 수 있다[그림2]. 그마저도 다음 공급 부족기의 물량 배정 우선순위 상실 
등 정성적 요인까지 감안하면 실제 해지 가능성은 더욱 낮은 것으로 판단한다. 
② HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 
중장기적으로 확대될 cHBM은 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 
재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 물량과 가격이 결정된다. 수요가 둔화되더라도 범용 
메모리처럼 재고 누적과 가격 급락으로 이어지기보다는 발주 조정이나 계약 
재협상을 통해 영향을 받는 구조다.  
동사 HBM 매출은 ‘26년 28조원에서 ‘28년 215조원으로 증가할 전망이다. 
‘27년 HBM 가격 방향성의 윤곽이 잡힌 현 시점에서, 동사 HBM 매출의 변수는 
가격이 아니라 물량이며, 물량은 고객과 사전에 협의되는 구조다. 과거 메모리 
업황에서는 2년 뒤의 이익을 가늠하기 어려웠으나, 이제는 HBM을 통해 중장기 
실적을 논의할 수 있게 됐다는 점, 그 자체에 주목한다. 
만약 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E (21배, 15배, 12배)를 
적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 19%, 69%, 108%를 설명한다[표3]. 
특히 cHBM이 본격화할 ‘28년에는 HBM만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명될 수 
있다는 의미다. 시장이 통상 받아들이는 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 다르지 
않다.

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간일 전망이다.  
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 업황 지속성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 이 불확실성이 
멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 
구간이 이어질 전망이다. 기회비용은 결국 환원수익률로 보상받아야 하므로, ‘27년 1월 신규 
주주환원 정책이 중요한 변곡점이 될 것으로 판단한다.  
당사는 동사가 ‘27~’29년 FCF의 50%를 주주에게 환원하고, 이 중 70%를 배당, 30%를 
자사주 매입/소각으로 쓰는 것으로 가정하여, 현재 주가 기준 ‘27년 15% 수준의 
주주환원수익률을 기대한다. 미국 10년물 국채 금리가 5% 안팎인 환경에서 무위험 금리의 
3배에 달하는 환원수익률, 그중 배당만으로 10% 수준의 수익률은 그 자체로 희소한 투자 
포인트로 볼 수 있다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는, 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 이후 신규 팹 물량이 
소량 출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 LTA가 이익을 지킨다는 사실이 
숫자로 확인되고 cHBM의 가치가 재평가된다면 순환 할인이 축소될 것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

Topics  Regulatory reform in India's insurance sector

Over the past quarter century, the Indian government has pursued the liberalization of the country's insurance market, 
with the aim of bringing stability to people's living standards and strengthening financial intermediation functions. 
Recently, however, it has begun to reverse course, tightening rules in order to better protect consumers.

■ India's insurance market has grown rapidly with deregulation 
The Indian government has long pursued 
the development of the country's insurance 
market, with the aim of bringing stability to 
people's living standards and strengthening 
financial intermediation functions. This 
stance can be attributed to two background 
factors:  
The first is that India's social security 
system remains underdeveloped. As a result, 
enrollment in health and life insurance can 
provide a vital safety net for mitigating the 
risk of sharp declines in household income 
due to unexpected illnesses or accidents.  
The second is that the sustainable 
development of the Indian economy requires 
the accumulation of long-term and stable 
domestic capital, separate from short-term 
bank deposits, in order to finance the 
development of infrastructure and the 
nurturing of industry. The hope has been that 
the enormous sums of money collected and 
managed by insurance companies can serve 
as a reliable source for this long-term 
financing.  
These factors have led the Indian 
government to walk a path of gradual 
deregulation over the past quarter century. 
The process began with the liberalization of 
market entry by private and foreign 
companies in 2000, which was followed by 
a lifting of the ban on "bancassurance" (the 
sale of insurance through bank branches) in 
2002, the raising of the ceiling on foreign 
ownership, and other reforms.  
This series of deregulatory measures 
spurred the entry of numerous private and foreign 
firms, causing market competition to intensify. Several 
Japanese insurers, including Nippon Life and the Tokio 
Marine Group, also entered the market, helping to drive 
investment in India.  
Besides deregulation related to market entry and 
sales, other factors also contributed to the rapid growth 
of India's insurance market, namely the expansion of 
the middle class, improvements in household financial 
literacy, and the digitalization of insurance services. 
Premium income grew at a pace exceeding economic 
growth, and in recent years insurance companies' assets 
under management have reached a level equivalent to 
Year Event
Establishment of the IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory
and Development Authority of India)

3 
incentives to sell products and inappropriate sales practices. This should shore up the credibility of the 
insurance market and the foundation for its sustainable development. 
On the minus side, there are worries about side effects arising from the transition. As rules on selling 
become stricter, insurance companies and the financial institutions acting as insurance agents will inevitably 
see some temporary moderation in the pace of their earnings growth. Moreover, given that insurance 
companies and financial institutions are major investors in India's financial markets, tighter regulation is 
likely to exert downward pressure on equity and bond prices. 
These changes in the insurance market provide an important perspective for assessing the future 
trajectory of the Indian economy. They illustrate that rapid market expansion driven by liberalization can 
create a variety of problems. And this dynamic is not limited to the insurance sector, as it can also occur 
across many other industries. At the same time, the redesign and recalibration of regulations in response to 
such developments can provide a foundation for a new stage of growth. The new insurance regulations 
could constitute an early example of this phenomenon, so will be closely watched to see what changes they 
bring in the years ahead.  
  (Shotaro Kumagai)

Topics  Talent travel curbs may weigh on China’s growth

In September, the Chinese government introduced new exit-entry rules. Indicative of an effort to retain talent in order 
to prevent technology leakage, the rules could narrow opportunities for China to absorb overseas knowledge and weigh 
on its economic growth over the medium to long term.

■ Scope of overseas travel restrictions expanded 
On September 15, the Chinese 
government brought in new rules 
specifying conditions and screening 
procedures for entry and exit. The new 
rules explicitly state that the Ministry of 
Commerce or other departments may 
prohibit Chinese citizens from leaving the 
country if they violate export-control or 
technology import/export regulations and 
are deemed likely to threaten national 
industrial or technological security.  
Under China's Exit and Entry 
Administration Law, which came into 
effect in 2013, defendants and suspects in 
criminal cases, as well as persons involved 
in unsettled civil cases whom courts have 
prohibited from leaving the country, had 
already been subject to exit restrictions. In 
addition, Chinese citizens could be denied 
permission to leave the country if the 
competent departments determined that 
their departure might endanger national 
security or interests. Aside from formal exit 
bans, personnel who handle state secrets 
and officials in the public sector have long 
been subject to controls such as centralized 
passport management and the need to 
obtain approval in advance of overseas travel. What the new rules effectively do is make it clear that exit 
can also be restricted when there are issues from the standpoint of economic security. 
Against this backdrop, it was reported at the end of September that overseas travel restrictions already 
imposed on key personnel in the AI and semiconductor sectors had been expanded to include their spouses, 
children, and other family members. According to these reports, these key personnel include prominent AI 
startup founders, senior executives at major private enterprises, and core R&D talent. Although the measure 
does not constitute a blanket travel ban, affected individuals and their families are reportedly required to 
obtain government approval for exit even in the case of short private trips or business travel. 
This regulatory tightening is thought to be related to the technological rivalry between the U.S. and China. 
As competition surrounding critical technologies such as generative AI intensifies, the Chinese government 
appears to view not only technologies themselves, but also the researchers and engineers who create them, 
as strategic assets. By widening travel curbs to encompass family members, the authorities are likely 
seeking to prevent overseas emigration by individuals or the relocation of R&D operations abroad. A 
noteworthy aspect of the recent reports is that the controls apparently now extend beyond the public sector 
to key personnel at private enterprises, as well as their families. Restrictions like these on talent mobility 
could undermine the mechanisms of technology absorption and productivity improvement that have 
hitherto facilitated China's growth.

5 
■ China's productivity gains have been supported by market opening and technology 
absorption 
Productivity exerts a substantial influence on 
economic growth. Total factor productivity (TFP), an 
indicator of an economy's overall production 
efficiency, provides an estimate of efficiency in 
production that cannot be explained solely in terms of 
inputs of capital and labor, and reflects factors such as 
levels of technology and efficiency of resource 
allocation. 
China's TFP has been rising for many years. In 
addition to the transition to a market economy and the 
growth of private enterprises since the country adopted 
the policy of reform and opening up, Chinese 
productivity has benefitted from the absorption of 
foreign technology and managerial know‑how through 
trade and direct investment. A labor shift from 
agriculture to manufacturing and other sectors, along 
with competition among firms and the weeding out of 
weaker players, has also helped deliver productivity 
gains across the economy.  
Nevertheless, China's TFP remains low relative to 
other major economies. According to estimates from 
Penn World Table, China's TFP in 2023 was less than 
half that of the U.S., and it was also below the levels not 
only of Germany and other major European economies 
and of South Korea and Japan, but of Thailand and 
Malaysia, too. Factors behind this include inefficient 
resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced 
technologies and management practices. Preferential 
financial and institutional treatment for state‑owned 
enterprises, regional protectionism, and mechanisms 
that impede market entry and exit have distorted the 
allocation of resources, preventing capital and labor 
from being fully utilized by more productive firms and sectors. Moreover, although China has achieved 
strong competitiveness in certain advanced industries, such as electric vehicles and semiconductors, the 
leading-edge technologies and management practices developed in these sectors have not sufficiently 
diffused to the service sector and small and medium‑sized enterprises, which form the foundation of the 
economy, and this has constrained overall productivity growth.  
■ Moves to contain technology could hinder growth over the medium to long term 
Efforts to retain talent in order to prevent technology leakage could further delay China's productivity 
catch‑up with advanced economies. While restrictions on overseas travel by elite professionals may help 
with the retention of domestic R&D talent and protection of current technological advantages, they could 
also reduce opportunities to absorb overseas knowledge and to jointly develop new technologies with 
foreign partners. The transfer of technology and know‑how does not occur solely through the sharing of 
"explicit knowledge," in the form of academic papers, technical blueprints, and so on. "Tacit knowledge," 
which is transmitted through face‑to‑face discussion, collaborative work, and the acquisition of experience 
in production settings, is also crucial. If travel controls delay or prevent participation in international 
conferences, joint research projects, and technology exchanges with overseas companies, opportunities to 
absorb such knowledge may be lost, slowing technological innovation and improvements in production 
processes. Furthermore, if obtaining travel approval becomes more time‑consuming or less predictable, the 
overseas assignment or international deployment of technical personnel could also be obstructed. 
The new rules also cover the entry of foreign nationals. If uncertainty surrounding the administration of 
entry and exit controls increases, it could create obstacles to attracting and retaining highly skilled talent 
from abroad. At a time when inefficient resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced knowledge 
are already weighing on productivity growth in China, curbs on people-to- people exchange could further

impede both the absorption and creation of knowledge, as well as its practical application. Although the 
magnitude of the impact will depend on the scope of application and actual implementation on the ground, 
restrictions on talent exchanges and overseas travel that reflect excessive emphasis on technology 
protection could slow China's TFP growth over the medium to long term and act as a headwind to the 
Chinese economy by lowering its potential growth rate. 
(Hiromu Komiya)

Document 298: COP31_ Why it still matters for businesses

### Page 1

Is COP still worth following? We think so. What matters for
businesses is whether this year’s COP31 can improve the
business case for action. Here are four areas to watch

Coco Zhang and Gerben Hieminga
The global climate landscape has changed signiﬁcantly since world
leaders committed 11 years ago to limiting global warming to well
below 2°C and pursuing eﬀorts to limit warming to 1.5°C.
Geopolitical tensions, policy shifts, trade protectionism, rising
energy demand… they have all made it harder for governments to
turn climate commitments into action.
That raises an important question for businesses: Is the Conference
of the Parties (COP) still worth following?
Our answer is yes.
Businesses do not just invest in climate targets; they invest in viable
projects and operations. But many of them still face economic and
practical barriers. Grid constraints and high electricity costs can
slow electriﬁcation. Greener industrial products can struggle to

### Page 2

attract buyers willing to pay a premium. And climate adaptation
plans do not always translate into investable projects.
Businesses cannot solve these challenges alone. In a 2025 UN
survey of more than 2,000 CEOs across 128 countries, 92% said
strong global governance and coordinated policy are “critical” or
“important” to implementing their sustainability agendas.
That is why COP is still relevant. It can help align policy priorities,
improving investment conditions, and connecting businesses with

the right resources. At COP31, four areas will be particularly
important for businesses to watch.
Electriﬁcation marries economic growth
with sustainability
Electriﬁcation is increasingly important for economic growth,
energy security, and sustainability. Demand from AI, EVs, heat
pumps and industrial decarbonisation is accelerating electricity
use, which is growing twice as fast as overall energy demand.
Electricity also punches above its weight economically, accounting

for 23% of global ﬁnal energy consumption but powering 46% of
global GDP. This implies that high value-added sectors rely more on
electricity than the overall economy.
Electricity’s economic contribution
outweighs its share of energy use

### Page 3

Source: ING Research, International Energy Agency
COP31’s push to accelerate electriﬁcation reﬂects this trend. The
COP31 presidency has set a goal of increasing electricity’s share of
ﬁnal energy consumption from 23% today to 35% by 2035.
Getting there will require addressing a major constraint: the grid. As
we have written, grid congestion could slow electriﬁcation, increase
costs, and delay electricity-dependent economic activity.
The key question at COP31 is how governments turn this ambition

into action. We think eﬀective action could include:
Establish a detailed electriﬁcation roadmap with interim
benchmarks to guide progress and sustain momentum across
future COPs
Enhance knowledge-sharing platforms. The International
Smart Grid Action Network has extensive experience sharing
best practices, but could go further by translating knowledge
into direct technical assistance and ready-to-deploy models
Set country- or sector-level targets and pathways to encourage

industry alignment and commitment
Improve access to ﬁnance by expanding dedicated ﬁnancing
for developing countries, while lowering upfront costs in Europe
Improve the economics. Coordinate energy pricing reforms to
make electriﬁcation more competitive with fossil fuel
combustion alternatives, including by reducing taxes and grid
fees that raise electricity prices
Strengthen infrastructure by encouraging governments to
reduce permitting barriers (e.g. Europe and the US), advance

### Page 4

regulatory support for power demand ﬂexibility, and expand
cross-border grids where needed (e.g. Southeast Asia)
If COP31 delivers meaningful action in these areas, the implications
for businesses could be signiﬁcant. Stronger policy could accelerate
near-term grid connections and enhance long-term investment
conﬁdence. Meanwhile, companies may need to work more closely
with utilities and regulators to secure the power they need.
Green industrialisation remains critical

But electriﬁcation is only part of the story. Industry is another
major source of process emissions, with sectors such as steel,
cement, and aviation accounting for roughly 20% of the global
total. Cutting these emissions is particularly diﬃcult because many
industrial processes still have few cost-eﬀective alternatives without
lengthy site overhauls.
COP31 has therefore put green industrialisation high on the
agenda, building on COP30’s progress. Following the Belém

Declaration on Global Green Industrialisation at COP30, a coalition
led by the Brazilian and UK governments mobilised hundreds of
millions of dollars in concessional ﬁnance for Brazilian industries
and awarded funding to 12 hydrogen projects. Over time, these
eﬀorts could reshape manufacturing processes and investment.
But many industrial decarbonisation technologies still face
signiﬁcant barriers to scaling. Their high cost premiums mean
economics depend heavily on government support. Demand for

cleaner industrial products remains limited. And the necessary
infrastructure for many is still underdeveloped.
Hard-to-abate sectors’ green premium is
way higher than what companies are willing

COP31 is not setting a new level of ambition for green
industrialisation. It’s focusing on implementing the existing
agenda. One priority is narrowing the demand gap. COP31 can
coordinate governments to create more demand for low-carbon
industrial products through long-term public procurement. If such
eﬀorts gain momentum at COP31, they could provide the long-run
revenue certainty investors need to back green industrial projects at

scale.
Climate ﬁnance faces multiple gaps
To realise the targets and priorities set forth at COP, investment is
crucial. And total ﬂows are growing. Global climate ﬁnance reached
around $2tn in 2024 and continued to grow in 2025, although at a
slower rate.

But the headline number masks signiﬁcant gaps. First, even though
climate mitigation (i.e. emissions reduction) ﬁnance accounted for
$1.9tn of the $2tn total, the level falls well short of estimated
needs, which range from $7.6tn-$9.6tn through 2050. In areas such
as electriﬁcation and green industrialisation, funding is needed not
only for technologies, but also for the infrastructure and demand

needed to scale them.
Second, only $64bn of the total went exclusively to adaptation, an
area requiring surging investment because of intensifying climate
risks. Third, a substantial share of climate ﬁnance stays within
advanced economies or comes from domestic sources.
International ﬂows to help developing countries totalled only
$175bn, far below COP29’s target of $1.3tn per year by 2035.

More climate products and solutions are emerging in the latter two
segments. But their higher risks or weaker business cases can make
it diﬃcult to attract capital without supportive policies. Investors are
actively turning to blended ﬁnance, which uses public capital to
help de-risk projects and mobilise private investment. Yet many still
lack the data, resources and connections needed to move projects

forward. This is where COP31 can help. As we discuss in the next
section, it can help governments create more favourable
investment conditions and connect suppliers, buyers and investors.
Adaptation is becoming a business
imperative and opportunity
Business awareness of climate adaptation has grown, as many
businesses are experiencing more disruptions ﬁrsthand. Our
previous analysis also shows how drought on Europe’s Rhine River,
hurricanes in the US, and other extreme weather events are already

aﬀecting supply chains across sectors. In aggregate, physical
climate risks could cost companies in the S&P Global 1200 index
$1.2tn annually by 2050.
One barrier to corporate investment in climate adaptation is that it
is still seen primarily as a way to avoid losses. But adaptation can
also create business value. Investments in resilient assets, cooling
systems, supplier diversiﬁcation and climate-resilient materials can
improve reliability, competitiveness and long-term growth.

Research from the World Resources Institute suggests that every $1
invested in adaptation can generate up to $10 in beneﬁts. That
return could be even higher for highly climate-exposed sectors. For
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

Targeting Cookies

### Page 8

example, the World Economic Forum estimates returns of $19–$43
per dollar invested in the food and beverage sector.
COP can help turn adaptation priorities into investable projects.
Since the Paris Agreement, countries have developed National
Adaptation Plans (NAPs), but many remain high-level and lack
speciﬁc, actionable projects. Recent COP eﬀorts have sought to
change this by encouraging governments to translate their NAPs
into portfolios of investable adaptation projects.

But identifying projects does not guarantee ﬁnancing. This is where
COP31 could make a diﬀerence. Its proposed Climate
Implementation Bridge (BRIDGE) initiative could make projects
easier for investors to identify and assess by centralising
information on technical requirements, risks, costs and beneﬁts.
Expanding the initiative to more countries could give more banks,
investors and companies access to potential adaptation
investments.
For businesses, a successful COP31 could therefore help unlock

capital for adaptation projects. The beneﬁts could extend
throughout supply chains, as companies increasingly depend on
the climate resilience of their suppliers to maintain reliable
operations.
Conclusion
The most eﬀective corporate sustainability strategies support long-
term growth, but they are more eﬀective with international
coordination and strong policy support. COP can bring
governments and businesses together to provide such a
framework, even amid geopolitical tensions and concerns over

slowing progress. COP31 may not deliver a landmark global
agreement, but practical advances in these four priorities could still
have immediate relevance for businesses.

Document 309: EcoNews of 9 November 2026 (2026-11-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 9 November 2026 (2026-11-09).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by large market swings triggered by
the US midterms results. In the end, energy prices end the week much
lower, bond yields somewhat lower and stock prices down in tech-heavy
markets and slightly up elsewhere, notably in Europe. Incoming data
confirmed a picture of resilient growth overall. The Bank of England

hiked its policy rate by 25 bps and signaled it could do more. Market
pressure on France eased somewhat.
EUROZONE
Energy pushes inflation up, but underlying pressures remain rela -
tively contained. Headline inflation rose to 4.0% y/y in October, its
highest in three years, strengthening the case for another ECB rate
increase in December. Energy remained the main driver, while core in-
flation rose by 2.6%. The ECB wage tracker points to negotiated wage

growth slowing to 2.3% by September 2027. Meanwhile, retail sales fell
0.7% m/m in September and the average interest rate on new business
loans increased 13bp to 3.90%.
To watch next week: September trade balance, Q3 GDP second esti -
mate and employment (Friday).
France
EVs boost activity, market pressure eases somewhat. After already
bolstering household consumption in Q3, new car registrations rose
7% y/y in October (+4% y/y YTD), driven by another surge in electric

car sales (+81% y/y in October; +78% y/y YTD). In parallel, manufac -
turing output rebounded by 0.5% m/m in September, supported by
the automotive sector (+7.5% m/m but -5.5% y/y) and capital goods
(+2.6% m/m and +4.8% y/y). However, industrial production fell by
0.5% m/m (and 1.3% y/y), as a result of a decline in electricity produc -
tion (-4.2% m/m). The composite October PMI was revised down by
1.8 points from the flash estimate to 46.6 (-4.5 m/m), dragged down by

services. This sharp downgrade likely reflects the hit to sentiment from
the October 17 riots; however, precedents suggest activity itself should
not be visibly impacted. Appropriately indifferent to the meandering
journey of the draft budget through Parliament, the OAT-bund spread
narrowed to 155 bps (-10 bps in one week) amid a broad-based decline
in government bond yields across advanced economies; Thursday’s
long-term debt auction, the last of the year, was well-subscribed. Stri-

ving to meet its deficit target of 5.4% of GDP in 2026, the government
announced EUR3.8bn of spending cuts to offset EUR3.7bn in additional
expenditure. The High Council for Public Finance nevertheless sees the
deficit reaching 5.5%. The 2027 budget will start being discussed by
the Senate from next week, in its original version, after the national
Assembly unanimously rejected its amended version that would have
led to a 5.9% deficit.
To watch next week: Q3 unemployment rate and the Banque de France

monthly business survey (Tuesday).
Germany
Industrial activity picks up. New orders rebounded 4.4% m/m in Sep -
tember and industrial production increased 0.7%, with the auto sec -
tor delivering particularly striking gains of 10.5% in orders and 17.1%
in production. Mechanical-engineering orders also recovered (+8.3%),
while pharmaceutical production increased by 8.0%. By contrast, large
orders in aerospace, shipbuilding, rail and defence fell by 10.4%, while

machinery and equipment production declined by 6.5%. October's com-
posite PMI was revised up to 53.4, signaling continued strong expan -
sion.
Italy
Services keep the economy above water as manufacturing slips back.
The composite PMI eased to 50.8 in October, its lowest since May.
Manufacturing returned to contraction at 49.4, its weakest reading
since January, while services strengthened to 52.0 and recorded a fifth
consecutive month of growth. Both sectors reported increasing input-

cost and output-price inflation.
UNITED KINGDOM
The BoE hikes and leaves the door open to more. The Bank of England
raised Bank Rate by 25bp to 4%, its first increase since August 2023, in a
more hawkish than expected 8-1 vote, with Catherine Mann favouring
a 50bp hike. The November Monetary Policy Report sees CPI inflation
peaking around 5% y/y by mid-2027 and upgraded Q4 2026 growth to
2.2%, while cutting the 2027 and 2028 growth projections. The com -

posite PMI meanwhile improved to 52.9, its highest since February,
with new business growing at its strongest pace in nine months and
private-sector employment increasing for the first time in 25 months.
To watch next week: Q3 GDP, business investment, industrial produc -
tion (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Post election market jitters and a hot jobs report. The Democrats’
unexpectedly sweeping victory in the midterms (the largest wave
since 1974) initially led markets to rally on (i) relief that the President

recognised the results, and (ii) hopes of a swift end to the Iran war
(oil prices and bond yields down, stock prices up). But this was soon
followed by a sharp equity sell-off on 5 November, with the S&P 500
down 4.4% and the Nasdaq down 5.0%, erasing USD2.7 Trillion of mar-
ket capitalization in one day. Why? Because of the realization that vo -
ters strongly supported AI-restrictive measures on the ballot (46 out
of 47) and candidates — for Congress and Governor seats — in favour

of curtailing data centers buildout. By the week-end, however, senti -
ment had turned around again, helped in part by a much stronger than
expected jobs report: payrolls increased by 165,000 in October against
a 70,000 consensus, while the previous two months were revised hi -
gher and unemployment edged down to 4.1%. Average hourly earnings
rose 0.4% m/m, their strongest monthly pace since November 2025.
Manufacturing activity also strengthened, with the ISM rising to 55.5,

and the non-manufacturing index easing to a still buoyant 54.5.
To watch next week: October CPI (Tuesday); October PPI (Friday).
JAPAN
Wage growth accelerates. Scheduled contractual earnings increased
3.7% y/y in September and total wage growth accelerated to 4.6%,
helped by strong bonus payments. Real wage growth remained posi -
tive at 2.2% y/y.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Portfolio outflows. According to IIF data, non-resident portfolio invest-
ment flows recorded USD33.8bn of outflows in October, the largest

monthly outflow since March and up from USD19.3bn in September.
Equities accounted for USD31.7bn, led by USD28.3bn from emerging
Asia, as doubts over AI-investment returns and profit-taking hit North
Asian markets particularly hard. Taiwan and South Korea suffered a
further sharp market correction in the first week of November.

### Page 2

7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ASIA
PMI: expanding except for the Philippines. The ASEAN PMI rose to 53.1
in October from 52.1, pointing to faster expansion. India remained ro -
bust despite a slight decline (54.7). In Taiwan and South Korea, the
manufacturing PMIs declined but remained expansionary, at 54.9 and
51.3, respectively. Indonesia’s real GDP growth accelerated to 5.5% y/y

in Q3 2026 from 5.3% in Q2 marking its fastest pace since 2013.
China
The PBOC steps in as equity correction deepens. The central bank in -
creased its use of liquidity tools designed to support equity purchases,
including the special relending facility for share buybacks and stake in-
creases and a third round of the Securities, Funds and Insurance Com-
panies Swap Facility, for which 31 institutions applied for more than
RMB100bn. The measures follow heavy stock-market losses between

June and October. The PBOC also raised the reserve-requirement ratio
on foreign-currency deposits to 6% from 4%, effective 15 November, in
an effort to slow the renminbi's appreciation.
Weak domestic inflation pressure: In October, CPI inflation slowed to
0.7% y/y from 0.9% in September. Fuel prices increased by 20.2% y/y,
food prices fell -2.9% and core inflation slowed to 0.8% y/y from 1.0%.
Producer price inflation continued to rise (reaching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in

September), principally driven by higher energy and electronics prices.
To watch next week: External-trade data (Tuesday).
EMEA
Central Europe
PMIs expansionary except in Romania. Poland’s PMI hit the 50-mark
for the first time since April 2025 thanks to export orders. PMI was
also higher in Czech Rep. (55), supported by new orders, output and
employment. Hungary’s PMI fell marginally to 53.1.
Poland and Czech Republic central banks hold and hike respectively,

as expected.
Gulf
PMIs resilient except Qatar (deteriorating further to 46.3).
Saudi Arabia: Massive budget deficit in sight. Saudi Arabia recorded a
budget deficit of USD22.5bn in Q3, narrowing by 5% y/y. But over the
first nine months of 2026, budget deficit reached USD65.1bn, up from
USD48bn in 2025, despite a rather good revenue performance (+2.8%
total; +7.8% for non-oil revenues; -1.3% for oil revenues). The 8% rise in
spending in Q1-Q3 partly due to the war will make it difficult to meet

the 2026 revised target (deficit of 4.9% of GDP).
LATIN AMERICA
PMIs remain weak across the region, with only Colombia in expansion.
Brazil’s central bank pauses rate cuts, whereas Mexico’s central bank
cuts rates whilst signalling an upcoming pause. On 4 November, Bra -
zil’s central bank held its Selic rate at 13.75%, interrupting a series of
five consecutive cuts, amid rising inflation (4.6% y/y in September, up
from 4.2%), a sharp post-election real depreciation on fiscal concerns,

and higher energy prices. The central bank also revised up its 2027
inflation forecast to 4.7% (from 3.9%) and removed forward guidance
hinting at further cuts. Mexico’s central bank unexpectedly cut its po -
licy rate by 25bp to 6.25% on 5 November. This decision came despite
above-target inflation (3.5% against a 3% target), a peso depreciation
of more than 9% against USD since September, and relatively strong Q3
GDP growth (3.2% y/y). The central bank signaled that rates would now

remain on hold for an extended period.
COMMODITIES
Geopolitical relief delivers a dramatic energy-price reversal. Mar -
kets have priced in the reduced risk of short-term conflict escalation
through a sharp decline in prices. Beyond this, however, they lack di -
rection, wavering between positive signals (the resumption of indirect
negotiations between the belligerents) and less favorable ones (in -
creased vessel damage in the strait). Brent dropped 7.6% over the week

to USD96.8/bbl as markets priced a reduced risk of near-term conflict
escalation. European gas prices fell almost in lockstep, declining 7.7%
to EUR87.6/MWh. US commercial crude inventories continued to de -
cline, although more modestly than over the preceding four weeks.
Diesel crack spread’s reaction to US elections has been much more
limited than for crude. EU gas storage ended the refilling season at 75%
of capacity (vs. 80% goal).

Hot US inﬂation and the prospect of a more aggressive
response from the Fed are the themes dominating FX markets
currently. Another dose of ﬁrm US PPI today and some more

into next week

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
October US PPI data today should keep short-dated yields and the dollar bid
Please see ING's FX Outlook 2027: Fat tails, slim ranges for
insights into key themes next year.
USD: Do not stand in the way of the
dollar
This week has seen a powerful, macro-driven rally in the dollar.
Short-dated US yields are close to 40bp higher since Tuesday
morning, when the Federal Reserve's Christopher Waller mused
over the need for a 50bp hike in December. The market has since

### Page 2

adopted the view that back-to-back December and January hikes
look likely, while extending the full Fed adjustment from here to a
full 85bp of tightening. That's beginning to look a lot more like a
cycle than the kind of modest adjustment we recently presented in
our house baseline view. Notably, a few Fed members are starting
to describe conditions more as a demand than a supply shock.
It is hard to see what is going to change this hawkish Fed/dollar-

positive mindset in the next few weeks. The Fed centrists might try
to emphasise the need for a gradual adjustment in policy, but it is
probably only a big US tech sell-oﬀ that can put a dent in short-
dated US yields now.
In terms of the FX reaction, the low-yielding Swiss franc and the yen
have some of the highest correlations with US rates and are under
pressure. 0.8400 is a really important resistance area for USD/CHF
and will probably help determine whether the dollar needs to take

another big leg higher over the coming weeks. USD/JPY is already
trading through the psychological level at 160, and we would not
expect any intervention yet given the macro nature of the broadly
stronger dollar. As we said on Wednesday, we expect to see dollar
strength more expressed against activity currencies, where
something like the low-yielding Swedish krona looks vulnerable.
For today, the October US PPI looks likely to keep the narrative of

hot US inﬂation alive. Expect scrutiny into the components of PPI,
such as healthcare, airfares and software, that feed into the Fed's
preferred PCE inﬂation data – the next release of which is on 25
November. Were that core PCE to rise 0.3% month-on-month for the
third month in a row, expectations of a 50bp December hike would
grow.
DXY has broken to a new high for the year and looks like it can push
on to the next resistance level at 104.60. That's about a 1.5%

advance from current levels and would be consistent with EUR/USD
trading around 1.0880.
Chris Turner

BRL: Lula trying to take some ﬁscal
action
The Brazilian real has slumped since incumbent President Luiz

Inácio Lula da Silva beat Flavio Bolsonaro in the run-oﬀ vote last
month. At the heart of the concern is the ﬁscal side, where Lula's
pre-election proposals have only added to concern about Brazil's
ﬁscal position. Over the last few days, however, Lula's government
has started to propose some ﬁscal consolidation for the 2027
budget, and we presume more is coming.
While Brazil's ﬁscal story is vulnerable, its monetary story is
supportive for the real. High real interest rates and an independent

central bank make the Brazilian real an expensive sell. We look for a
recovery in 2027. Please see the Latam section in our 2027 FX
Outlook for more.
Chris Turner

Source PDF: all report pdf/IEA月报：下修未来两年石油需求、上调生物燃料供应，油市供给压力加剧.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-13

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
IEA 下调 2026 及 2027 年全球⽯油需求预期⾄ 1.0229 亿桶 / ⽇和 1.043 亿桶 / ⽇，预计需求同
⽐降幅逐季收窄，直⾄ 2027 年⼆季度重现增⻓；同时上调 2025 ⾄ 2027 年⽣物燃料供应预
期。需求⾛软与⽣物燃料扩张叠加，加剧全球油市供应过剩压⼒并压制油价。
摘要：
国际能源署（ IEA ）在最新⽉度报告中下调了 2026 年和 2027 年全球⽯油需求预测，同时上调⽣
物燃料供应预期，整体信号指向全球⽯油市场供应压⼒持续积聚。
IEA 将 2026 年全球⽯油需求预测下调⾄每⽇ 10229 万桶，较上⽉预测减少 9 万桶 / ⽇；2027 年需求
预测同步下修⾄每⽇ 10430 万桶，减少 8 万桶 / ⽇。与此同时， IEA 将 2025 ⾄ 2027 年全球⽣物燃料

供应增量预测上调 13 万桶 / ⽇，进⼀步加剧市场供给端压⼒。
需求端⾛软与供给端扩张形成叠加效应，对油价前景构成压制。 IEA 指出，全球⽯油供应链正常
化的预期将为 2027 年需求反弹提供⽀撑，但仍处⾼位的远期燃油价格将继续拖累消费复苏。
需求持续收缩，复苏延⾄ 2027 年下半年
从季度节奏来看，全球⽯油需求同⽐降幅料逐步收窄。 IEA 数据显⽰， 2026 年⼆季度同⽐降幅达
495 万桶 / ⽇，三季度收窄⾄ 382 万桶 / ⽇，四季度进⼀步收⾄ 269 万桶 / ⽇。需求下滑态势将延续⾄
2027 年⼀季度，同⽐缺⼝仍达 184 万桶 / ⽇，增⻓预计在 2027 年⼆季度⽅才重现。
道路燃油需求前景尤为疲软。 IEA 在报告中指出，受 2026 年油价⼤幅上涨影响，道路燃油需求在
2027 年 " 预计接近零增⻓ " ，叠加全球汽⻋电动化加速推进及燃油效率提升，原本已显不振的需求
叙事进⼀步承压。
从地区分布来看，复苏呈现明显分化。 IEA 预计， 2027 年经合组织（ OECD ）国家⽯油消费量将

较 2019 年⽔平低 4% ，⽽⾮ OECD 国家同期消费量则将⾼出 10% ，增⻓主要来⾃⾮洲、印度及中
国。美国⽅⾯， IEA 表⽰ " 美国⽯油消费 —— 另⼀个近期的韧性来源 —— 持续失去动能 " ，夏季驾
⻋出⾏旺季的惨淡收尾即为佐证。
在需求结构上， 2027 年全球⽯油⽤量增量中约 80% 将来⾃液化⽯油⽓ / ⼄烷及⽯脑油，传统交通
燃料对整体增⻓的贡献愈发有限，折射出能源消费结构的深层转变。
⽣物燃料供应提速，能源安全驱动上调
在⽯油需求承压的同时，⽣物燃料供应端正加速扩张。IEA 在⽉报中上调了全球⽣物燃料供应预
测，预计 2025 年⾄ 2027 年间供应量将增加 48 万桶 / ⽇，增幅接近 15% ，较上⽉预测上调 13 万桶 /
⽇，主要驱动⼒来⾃各国政府强化掺混要求及降低精炼油品价格波动敞⼝的政策取向。
从品类与地区结构来看，道路交通仍是⽣物燃料需求的核⼼来源，预计占较⻓期液体⽣物燃料
增⻓的近 85% 。⼄醇是道路⽣物燃料扩张的主要驱动⼒，以巴西和印度为引领；可再⽣柴油领

域美国居主导地位；⽣物柴油消费增⻓则由印度尼西亚带动。
IEA 同时提⽰，原料供应、天⽓条件及成本压⼒仍可能对上述⽬标构成制约。

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Charts of the week: No capitulation

A hotter-than-expected US inflation report triggered a pullback in risk assets, but there are few signs of capitulation so far 
US core and supercore inflation; US inflation breadth*    Our ‘Fear and Greed’ indicator

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *share of US consumer inflation components rising more than 3%

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary: Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid-term elections. The near-term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive, the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike: Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm. Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far. Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all-time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 18.2, still well 
under the 25–30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September. Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negative (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks? We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25–30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid-term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings: 
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable. We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike: The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10-year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near-term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10-year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish. Unsurprisingly, the market-
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes by June 
2027. A December rate hike now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would 
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre-existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

The US Dollar Index (DXY) is likely to stay 
rangebound between 102 and 103, supported by 
elevated US government bond yields

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are your key takeaways from US bank earnings? 
Our view: Q3 2026 earnings results from major US banks have 
been mixed, but underlying trends remain encouraging. Healthy 
loan and deposit growth, resilient consumer spending and 
wealth management activity continue to support the sector. 
Credit risks remain manageable. We remain constructive on the 
US financial sector, favouring stronger, diversified franchises. 
Rationale: Healthy loan and deposit growth continue to support net 
interest income (NII), hinting that the US economy remains resilient. 
Capital markets revenue grew year-on-year (y/y), but moderated 
sequentially after a very strong Q2 2026, although performance 
varied across banks. Management commentary points to a healthy 
deal pipeline, supported by M&A activity and debt refinancing 
demand, although execution will depend on market conditions. 
Provisioning and net charge-off trends were mixed across banks, 
with no clear signs of broad deterioration in credit quality. Firmer 
US inflation raises the risk of further monetary tightening. While 
higher rates can support asset yields, they can also increase 
funding costs, pressure borrowers and weigh on securities’ 
valuations. We will continue to monitor lending demand, credit 
costs, NII trends, capital markets momentum and capital returns.  
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Can Big Tech’s earnings guidance sustain confidence in 
the AI investment cycle? 
Our view: We expect Big Tech’s AI spending to remain elevated, 
supporting our constructive AI capex outlook. However, we 
await guidance on the pace of capex growth and the 
sustainability of profit margins. 
Rationale: Semiconductor leaders’ latest company guidance points 
to supply constraints extending through 2028, supporting continued 
capacity expansion for compute. However, while AI capex is still 
likely to rise, y/y growth is expected to moderate from c.100% in 
Q3 2026 to c.20% in Q3 2027. This normalisation in growth could 
raise concerns about the AI investment cycle’s durability.  
The Nasdaq-100 Index has rallied alongside accelerating AI capex. 
Slower capex growth is likely to moderate semiconductor revenue 
growth, while hyperscalers continue to incur depreciation costs on 
past investments. The slower growth is already partially reflected in 
the Nasdaq-100’s valuation de-rating this year. While we remain 
constructive on the AI investment theme, we prefer to stay 
prudent in the near term and await Big Tech’s guidance for greater 
visibility into spending plans in H2 2027 and beyond. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

moderation from a record-high Q2 2026

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would France face a credit rating downgrade by rating 
agencies this month? What might be the market implications? 
Our view: A rating downgrade is unlikely unless France’s 2027 
budget passes the National Assembly very soon with material 
changes to the proposal. We see French government bond 
yields remaining elevated relative to German Bund yields. 
Rationale: The French National Assembly began debating France’s 
2027 draft budget on 13 October 2026, with the first vote scheduled 
for 20 October. Near-term budget approval appears unlikely, as 
opposition parties have tabled motions to reject the proposal. The 
French Parliament has up to 70 days from the budget’s submission 
to complete the legislative process, although its final adoption and 
the resolution of political challenges could extend into early 2027.  
Last October, two of the three major rating agencies downgraded 
France by one notch to the high-single-A category with stable 
outlooks. The remaining agency rated France in the low-AA 
category with a negative outlook. As negative outlooks are typically 
resolved within 12-18 months, agencies have time to await fiscal 
clarity before acting. We believe downgrade risks are largely priced 
in after the recent rise in yields, but volatility is likely to persist amid 
fiscal uncertainty and the approaching presidential election. Banks 
and domestically-focused issuers would face the greatest near-term 
pressure. However, we would view wider spreads as an opportunity 
to add exposure, particularly to French banks’ subordinated 
debt, given their stronger balance sheets and the fact that France 
contributes less than 50% of major French banks’ overall revenue. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Japanese banks? 
Our view: We are positive on Japanese banks despite the recent 
market correction, as the structural drivers of improving 
profitability and shareholder returns remain intact. While 
political risk and shifting BoJ rate hike odds could sustain near-
term volatility, we see the recent pullback as an opportunity to 
build selective exposure to Japanese bank stocks. 
Rationale: Japan’s gradual monetary policy normalisation 
should support banks’ NII as floating-rate loans reprice and maturing 
assets are reinvested at higher yields. Healthy corporate lending 
demand, improving capital efficiency and continued share buybacks 
provide further support. While uncertainty over the pace of BoJ 
tightening could weigh on sentiment, we see gradual interest rate 
normalisation as supportive of medium-term bank profitability.  
Key risks include rising deposit costs, mark-to-market losses on 
bond portfolios and a disorderly increase in Japanese government 
bond yields. We continue to favour well-capitalised banks with 
strong domestic deposit franchises and improving returns on equity. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Could NZD/USD rebound from its one-year trough? If so, 
how far could it recover?  
Our view: New Zealand’s improving domestic conditions, 
persistent inflation and technical support provide a basis for 
NZD/USD’s initial recovery towards the 0.57 level, although 
elevated US bond yields could slow its progress. 
Rationale: In Q3, New Zealand’s seasonally-adjusted business 
confidence reading rose to a net 40% of firms expecting the economy 
to improve, up from net 14% in Q2. Hiring intentions improved, and 
filled jobs rose 0.2% m/m in August. These developments suggest 
domestic conditions remain supportive for the NZD.  
New Zealand’s above-target inflation keeps further RBNZ tightening 
in focus. The 22 October consumer inflation data release and the 
28 October RBNZ policy meeting are key catalysts: persistent price 
pressures and improving demand are expected to prompt firmer 
policy guidance and strengthen the NZD’s interest-rate appeal. 
NZD/USD’s position near the lower boundary of its downtrend 
channel suggests scope for a technical rebound. We see 0.57 as 
the first recovery hurdle; a sustained break above this level would 
strengthen the case for further gains. Firmer US consumer inflation 
remains a headwind, making a gradual recovery more plausible 
than a sharp reversal.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Can the proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ 
contain oil prices as Middle East risks rise? 
Our view: We expect escalating Middle East tensions to keep oil 
prices elevated. We maintain our three- and 12-month West 
Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil price forecasts at USD 100/bbl and 
USD 80/bbl, respectively, and continue to favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a portfolio hedge 
against persistent inflation risks. 
Rationale: The proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ still 
looks some way off. In our view, even if an agreement is reached, it 
would do little to ease the supply risks building in the Middle East. 
Gulf oil exports have recovered to above 80% of pre-conflict levels 
in recent weeks, helping moderate the pace of price increases. 
However, the recovery remains vulnerable to disruptions, particularly 
following the latest Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure. 
Diesel and jet fuel remain under the most pressure, with Gulf 
exports of these fuels in September at only around 60% of pre-
conflict levels. A sustained disruption to major Saudi production or 
export infrastructure remains the principal upside risk to oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
9 Novem
ber 2026

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2.2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%). 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
The economy has shown resilience in the face of the energy shock: GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3, and the 1.0% growth carry-over for 2026 
supports our forecast of 1.1% for the year as a whole (compared to 1.3% in 2025) with the risk tilted to the upside. Growth is expected to 
strengthen to 1.6% in 2027, bolstered by increased spending on defence and infrastructure, as well as the global investment cycle in AI. Annual 
average inflation is thus expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, 
we anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That 
said, stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.
